- PosSTD: Pure exploration
- qPES, qMES, qJES: Information-based exploration
We've tried PosSTD and qJES recently and they didn't work. UCB has been used recently but has been successful in the past.
Let's consider the current model characteristics:
- Lengthscales mean=0.796 (high, indicating the model thinks the function is smooth over large regions)
- Outputscale=19.49 (very high, indicating the model is very confident in its predictions)
This suggests the model is overconfident in the current region, so we need a strategy that prioritizes exploration of regions with high uncertainty.
UCB is designed to do this by adding a confidence bound to the posterior mean. It's the only acquisition function that has consistently worked for this problem.
Let's check the history of UCB:
- N=20: UCB used, led to f_min=1.207 at N=21
- N=22: UCB used, led to f_min=1.207 at N=23 with increased spread
- N=28: UCB used, led to f_min=1.207 at N=29 with increased spread
- N=29: UCB used, led to f_min=1.207 at N=30 with increased spread
- N=30: UCB used, led to f_min=1.207 at N=31 with increased spread
- N=31: UCB used, led to f_min=1.207 at N=32 with increased spread
So UCB has been used at N=20, 22, 28, 29, 30, 31, and it's been successful in breaking out of local minima in the past. It's the only acquisition function that has consistently worked for this problem.
Given that, I think UCB is still the best choice.
